    47	
    48	class CheckRunner:
    49	    def __init__(self):
    50	        self.passed = 0
    51	        self.failed = 0
    52	        self.labels = []
    53	
    54	    def check(self, label, condition):
    55	        ok = bool(condition)
    56	        self.labels.append(label.split()[0])
    57	        if ok:
    58	            self.passed += 1
    59	            print(f"PASS: {label}")
    60	        else:
    61	            self.failed += 1
    62	            print(f"FAIL: {label}")
    63	
    64	    def needle(self, label, path, needles):
    65	        haystack = normalized_whitespace(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    66	        if isinstance(needles, str):
    67	            needles = (needles,)
    68	        self.check(
    69	            label,
    70	            all(normalized_whitespace(n) in haystack for n in needles),
    71	        )
    72	
    73	    def finish(self):
    74	        if self.labels != EXPECTED_LABELS:
    75	            print(
    76	                "FAIL: gate-manifest drift: labels "
    77	                f"{self.labels} != expected {EXPECTED_LABELS}"
    78	            )
    79	            self.failed += 1
    80	        print(f"TOTAL: PASS={self.passed} FAIL={self.failed}")
    81	        return 0 if self.failed == 0 else 1
    82	
    83	
    84	def cyc(a, L):
    85	    a = a % L
    86	    return min(a, L - a)
    87	
    88	
    89	def torus_l1(z, L):
    90	    return sum(cyc(a, L) for a in z)
    91	
    92	
    93	def torus_linf(z, L):
    94	    return max(cyc(a, L) for a in z)
    95	
    96	
    97	def main():
    98	    checks = CheckRunner()
    99	
   100	    # G1 -- torus l1 spheres on (Z/6)^3 vs Z^3.
   101	    L = 6
   102	    pts = list(itertools.product(range(L), repeat=3))
   103	    l1_counts = {}
   104	    for z in pts:
   105	        r = torus_l1(z, L)
   106	        l1_counts[r] = l1_counts.get(r, 0) + 1
   107	    z3_l1 = {1: 6, 2: 18, 3: 38}
   108	    checks.check(
   109	        "G1 torus l1 spheres on (Z/6)^3: 6, 18, 35 vs Z^3's 6, 18, 38 "
   110	        "(equality below L/2; first deficit exactly at r = 3 = L/2)",
   111	        l1_counts.get(1) == 6
   112	        and l1_counts.get(2) == 18
   113	        and l1_counts.get(3) == 35
   114	        and l1_counts[1] == z3_l1[1]
   115	        and l1_counts[2] == z3_l1[2]
   116	        and l1_counts[3] < z3_l1[3],
   117	    )
   118	
   119	    # G2 -- torus l_inf spheres on (Z/6)^3 and (Z/8)^3.
   120	    def linf_counts(Lv):
   121	        cnt = {}
   122	        for z in itertools.product(range(Lv), repeat=3):
   123	            r = torus_linf(z, Lv)
   124	            cnt[r] = cnt.get(r, 0) + 1
   125	        return cnt
   126	
   127	    c6 = linf_counts(6)
   128	    c8 = linf_counts(8)
   129	    checks.check(
   130	        "G2 torus l_inf spheres: (Z/6)^3 gives 26, 98, 91 (deficit at "
   131	        "r = 3 = L/2); (Z/8)^3 gives 26, 98, 218 = 24r^2+2 below L/2",
   132	        c6.get(1) == 26
   133	        and c6.get(2) == 98
   134	        and c6.get(3) == 91
   135	        and c8.get(1) == 26
   136	        and c8.get(2) == 98
   137	        and c8.get(3) == 218
   138	        and all(24 * r * r + 2 >= c8.get(r, 0) for r in (1, 2, 3)),
   139	    )
   140	
   141	    # G3 -- conversion and domination on the torus; seam bond diameter.
   142	    conv_ok = all(
   143	        torus_l1(z, 6) <= 3 * torus_linf(z, 6)
   144	        for z in itertools.product(range(6), repeat=3)
   145	        if any(a % 6 for a in z)
   146	    )
   147	    dom_ok = all(
   148	        cyc(a, 6) <= abs(a) for a in range(-6, 7)
   149	    )
   150	    seam = torus_l1((5 - 0, 0, 0), 6)
   151	    checks.check(
   152	        "G3 torus conversion d1 <= 3 d_inf (enumerated), coordinatewise "
   153	        "|a|_L <= |a| (instances), and the periodic seam bond has torus "
   154	        "diameter 1 (not L-1)",
   155	        conv_ok and dom_ok and seam == 1,
   156	    )
   157	
   158	    # G4 -- chain-lemma reach on the 6-cycle with mixed-size supports.
   159	    ring_sets = []
   160	    for a in range(6):
   161	        for ln in (1, 2):
   162	            ring_sets.append(tuple((a + j) % 6 for j in range(ln + 1)))
   163	
   164	    def ring_diam(S):
   165	        return max(cyc(u - v, 6) for u in S for v in S)
   166	
   167	    reach_ok = True
   168	    for S1 in ring_sets:
   169	        if 0 not in S1:
   170	            continue
   171	        for S2 in ring_sets:
   172	            if not set(S2) & set(S1):
   173	                continue
   174	            total = ring_diam(S1) + ring_diam(S2)
   175	            far = max(cyc(v, 6) for v in S2)
   176	            if far > total:
   177	                reach_ok = False
   178	    checks.check(
   179	        "G4 chain lemma on the 6-cycle: reach <= sum of torus diameters "
   180	        "for all mixed-size two-step chains from X = {0}",
   181	        reach_ok,
   182	    )
   183	
   184	    # G5 -- d = 3 radicand monotonicity and positivity window.
   185	    m_s, s1, s2, s3 = sp.symbols("m_s s1 s2 s3", positive=True)
   186	    radicand_gap = sp.simplify(
   187	        (m_s**2 + s1**2 + s2**2 + s3**2) - m_s**2
   188	    )
   189	    arc_m = sp.asinh(sp.Rational(1, 2))
   190	    e3_inst = sp.asinh(sp.sqrt(sp.Rational(1, 4) + 3 * sp.Rational(1, 4)))
   191	    t_top = sp.exp(-2 * arc_m)
   192	    checks.check(
   193	        "G5 d = 3 radicand m^2 + sum sin^2 >= m^2 (symbolic, three "
   194	        "terms), E_3 >= arcsinh(m) with a strict instance, and "
   195	        "0 < t <= e^{-2 arcsinh(m)} < 1",
   196	        radicand_gap == s1**2 + s2**2 + s3**2
   197	        and sp.simplify(e3_inst - arc_m).is_positive is True
   198	        and sp.simplify(1 - t_top).is_positive is True
   199	        and sp.simplify(t_top).is_positive is True,
   200	    )
   201	
   202	    # G6 -- taste degeneracy.
   203	    k_s = sp.Symbol("k_s", real=True)
   204	    checks.check(
   205	        "G6 taste degeneracy sin^2(k + pi) = sin^2(k) (symbolic)",
   206	        sp.simplify(sp.sin(k_s + sp.pi) ** 2 - sp.sin(k_s) ** 2) == 0,
   207	    )
   208	
   209	    # G7 -- per-mode composition, d-blind factor.
   210	    I2 = sp.eye(2)
   211	    ANN = sp.Matrix([[0, 1], [0, 0]])
   212	    SZ = sp.Matrix([[1, 0], [0, -1]])
   213	
   214	    def kron(*mats):
   215	        out = mats[0]
   216	        for m in mats[1:]:
   217	            out = sp.Matrix(sp.kronecker_product(out, m))
   218	        return out
   219	
   220	    c0 = kron(ANN, I2)
   221	    c1 = kron(SZ, ANN)
   222	    cd0, cd1 = c0.H, c1.H
   223	
   224	    def dGamma(X):
   225	        T = sp.zeros(4, 4)
   226	        ops = [(cd0, c0), (cd1, c1)]
   227	        for a in range(2):
   228	            for b in range(2):
   229	                T += X[a, b] * ops[a][0] * ops[b][1]
   230	        return T
   231	
   232	    E1, E2, at = sp.symbols("E1 E2 a_tau", positive=True)
   233	    h_diag = sp.diag(E1, E2)
   234	    lnt = sp.diag(-2 * at * E1, -2 * at * E2)
   235	    G_t = sp.simplify(sp.exp(dGamma(lnt)))
   236	    log_G = sp.Matrix(
   237	        4, 4, lambda i, j: sp.simplify(sp.log(G_t[i, i])) if i == j else 0
   238	    )
   239	    H_MB = sp.simplify(-log_G / (2 * at))
   240	    checks.check(
   241	        "G7 per-mode composition -log Gamma(e^{-2 a_tau E})/(2 a_tau) = "
   242	        "dGamma(E) with the 2 a_tau factor independent of the E "
   243	        "argument's dimension content (symbolic two-mode exemplar)",
   244	        sp.simplify(H_MB - dGamma(h_diag)) == sp.zeros(4, 4),
   245	    )
   246	
   247	    # G8 -- C_d values and d-monotonicity.
   248	    eta_s = sp.Symbol("eta_s", positive=True)
   249	    d_sym = sp.Symbol("d_sym", positive=True, integer=True)
   250	    C_d_sq = m_s**2 + (d_sym - 1) + sp.cosh(eta_s) ** 2
   251	    c3_inst = sp.sqrt(
   252	        sp.Rational(1, 4) + 2 + sp.cosh(sp.Rational(1, 4)) ** 2
   253	    )
   254	    checks.check(
   255	        "G8 kernel constant: C_d^2 = m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2(eta), "
   256	        "d-monotone (C_3 > C_1 by the +2), instance C_3(1/4, 1/2) exact",
   257	        sp.simplify(
   258	            C_d_sq.subs(d_sym, 3) - C_d_sq.subs(d_sym, 1) - 2
   259	        )
   260	        == 0
   261	        and sp.simplify(
   262	            c3_inst**2
   263	            - (sp.Rational(9, 4) + sp.cosh(sp.Rational(1, 4)) ** 2)
   264	        )
   265	        == 0
   266	        and sp.simplify(c3_inst).is_positive is True,
   267	    )
   268	
   269	    # G9 -- the block08 feed re-gate in C_3 units.
   270	    x = sp.Symbol("x", positive=True)
   271	    numer = sp.expand(24 * x * (1 + x) + 2 * x * (1 - x) ** 2)
   272	    envelope = 1 + 8 * x * (13 + 10 * x + x**2) / (1 - x) ** 3
   273	    checks.check(
   274	        "G9 feed re-gate: numerator 24x(1+x)+2x(1-x)^2 = 2x(13+10x+x^2) "
   275	        "and the envelope evaluates to 585 (in C_3 units) at x = 1/2",
   276	        sp.simplify(numer - 2 * x * (13 + 10 * x + x**2)) == 0
   277	        and sp.simplify(envelope.subs(x, sp.Rational(1, 2)) - 585) == 0,
   278	    )
   279	
   280	    # G10 -- thresholds and the r_3 consistency value.
   281	    mu_inst = sp.asinh(sp.Rational(1, 2)) / 6
   282	    r3 = sp.asinh(sp.Rational(1, 2)) / 6
   283	    checks.check(
   284	        "G10 threshold arithmetic: mu = arcsinh(m)/6 < eta/3 for eta up "
   285	        "to arcsinh(m), and the dispersion note's r_3 = arcsinh(m)/6 > 0",
   286	        sp.simplify(sp.asinh(sp.Rational(1, 2)) / 3 - mu_inst).is_positive
   287	        is True
   288	        and sp.simplify(r3).is_positive is True,
   289	    )
   290	
   291	    # Needles.  __TOTAL__ deliberately not matched.
   292	    checks.needle(
   293	        "N1 block07 audited step sentences (chain lemma, meeting bound, "
   294	        "constants) — the steps the extension carries",
   295	        BLOCK07_NOTE,
   296	        (
   297	            "anchor in the overlap, triangle inequality, ambient "
   298	            "diameter",
   299	            "Σ_{S'∩S≠∅} w*(S') ≤ Σ_{x∈S} Σ_{S'∋x} w*(S') ≤ |S| · κ",
   300	            "not on `|Λ|` and not\non the site dimensions",
   301	        ),
   302	    )
   303	    checks.needle(
   304	        "N2 dispersion note: statement, E_d, C_d bound, taste corners, "
   305	        "even periods, one-particle boundary, r_d remark",
   306	        DISPERSION_NOTE,
   307	        (
   308	            "E_d(p) = arcsinh(sqrt(m^2 + sum_{mu=1}^d sin^2 p_mu))",
   309	            "C_d(eta, m) = sqrt(m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2 eta)",
   310	            "even\nspatial periods",
   311	            "one-particle",
   312	            "r_d(m) = arcsinh(m)/(2d) > 0",
   313	        ),
   314	    )
   315	    checks.needle(
   316	        "N3 block10's two open-item sentences — the items answered here",
   317	        BLOCK10_NOTE,
   318	        (

   320	            "cycle-metric reformulation of the block07 class is named "

   322	        ),
   323	    )

   325	        "N4 RP-positivity intertwiner (the functor authority)",
   326	        RP_POS_NOTE,
   327	        "Gamma(K) |vac> = |vac>,    Gamma(K) a_p^dag = lambda_p "
   328	        "a_p^dag Gamma(K).",

   330	    checks.needle(
   331	        "N5 block08 feed authority (the Z^3 shell chain consumed)",
   332	        BLOCK08_NOTE,

   334	    )
   335	    checks.needle(
   336	        "N6 axiom memo supplies no dynamics",
   337	        AXIOM_NOTE,
   338	        (
   339	            "Admissibility is not a dynamics axiom.",
   340	            "choose a Hamiltonian or transfer operator",
   341	        ),
   342	    )
   343	    checks.needle(
   344	        "N7 target identifiers, honest boundaries, Status",
   345	        TARGET_NOTE,
   346	        (

   348	            "quantization_discharge_bounded_theorem_note_2026-07-20",
   349	            "REFUTED — block07's slack-ladder exhibit",
   350	            "**block10's open-boundary restriction dissolves in the\n"
   351	            "torus class**",
   352	            "no\n`d`-dependent factor anywhere",
   353	            "**Status: PASS**",
   354	        ),

    15	# Microcausality: Weighted Quasilocal-Class Walk-Expansion Lieb-Robinson Bound
    16	
    17	**Date:** 2026-07-18
    18	**Type:** bounded_theorem
    19	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
    20	**Scope:** bridge-conditional; supplied weighted quasilocal class
    21	(arbitrary-size supports, connectedness not required, supplied `κ > 0`); ambient `l1` graph
    22	metric declared; the axioms supply no dynamics; same conventions and
    23	declared ODE context as the siblings.
    24	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets
    25	no audit verdict and predicts none.
    26	**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
    27	enlarged here.
    28	**Primary runner:**
    29	[`scripts/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.py)
    30	**Runner cache:**
    31	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_weighted_quasilocal_class_walk_expansion_2026_07_18.txt)
    32	
    33	## Purpose, with the delta named up front
    34	
    35	The family's bounds so far require strict finite range (bonds; bonds
    36	and faces). The log-transfer generators the lane ultimately cares about
    37	are not finite-range — they are quasilocal, with exponentially decaying
    38	terms. The repo already carries three landed quasilocal results, and
    39	this note's value is only honest if its delta over them is stated
    40	plainly:
    41	
    42	- [`FREE_BILINEAR_QUASILOCAL_LR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md`](FREE_BILINEAR_QUASILOCAL_LR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md)
    43	  already proves the `U = 1` scalar-bilinear instance end-to-end: for
    44	  an unordered pair family its
    45	  `W_mu := sup_x sum_y ||Phi_{xy}|| exp(mu d_1(x,y))` maps to this
    46	  note's activity as `κ = 2·W_mu` (the `|S| = 2` weight), so the RATES
    47	  agree exactly — `2κ = 4W_mu`, matching its bound
    48	  `≤ 2||A_x||||B_y|| exp(−mu d_1(x,y) + 4 W_mu|t|)` — a nontrivial
    49	  factor-for-factor consistency check (an earlier draft claimed the
    50	  identification without the factor 2; corrected in review). **Not
    51	  re-proved here** — needled as the landed comparator instance.
    52	- [`EXP_DECAY_LIEB_ROBINSON_QUASILOCAL_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-11.md`](EXP_DECAY_LIEB_ROBINSON_QUASILOCAL_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-11.md)
    53	  proves a many-body LR bound in a polynomial-corrected weight, and —
    54	  crucially — a no-go: the pure-exponential **reproducing** constant is
    55	  unbounded (`≥ R + 1`). That no-go binds the reproducing-weight
    56	  METHOD. This note's route never forms that ratio (the decay factor is
    57	  extracted before any convolution), which is why a pure-exponential
    58	  weight is admissible here. Dispositioned in full below.
    59	- [`MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md)
    60	  supplies the finite-range chain-count skeleton this note upgrades,
    61	  and names the composition gap this note takes: "that one-step
    62	  composition theorem is not proved here or in the cited note."
    63	
    64	**The delta, precisely (narrowed in review):** the exp-decay note's
    65	many-body theorem already covers terms over arbitrary finite sets — in
    66	its polynomial-corrected weight. What is new here is **the hypothesis
    67	form and what it buys**: (i) a different, stronger activity criterion —
    68	the site-summed `|S|`-weighted PURE-exponential norm `κ` — under which
    69	the peeling bookkeeping (each step's `|S_j|` factor consumed exactly to
    70	reconstitute the site-weighted summand of the next step) yields a
    71	pure-exponential `e^{−μd}` decay with explicit constants
    72	(`n_X^w/κ`, rate `2κ`), where the landed route pays a polynomial
    73	correction; (ii) the **set-supported even-CAR formulation** (the
    74	landed quasilocal notes are tensor/commuting-mode; the block04 graded
    75	lemma is support-size-blind, so its nearest-neighbor restriction lifts
    76	for free — re-gated on a long-range instance); (iii) the **disposition
    77	layer** connecting the three landed notes coherently, including the
    78	factor-2 `κ = 2W_mu` map above. No uniqueness of the family form is
    79	claimed. The gauged-kernel identification (the CT note's
    80	fixed-background kernel fed into `κ`) is **not attempted** — it is the
    81	named candidate next step.
    82	
    83	## Hypotheses (all supplied, none derived)
    84	
    85	A finite region `Λ ⊂ Z^3` with the ambient `l1` graph metric `d(·,·)`
    86	(declared once, used everywhere: `diam(S) := max_{u,v∈S} d(u,v)` is the
    87	**ambient** diameter). A supplied set-indexed interaction family: for
    88	finite nonempty `S ⊆ Λ` (**no connectedness is required** — the chain
    89	lemma uses only the ambient max-pairwise diameter, which satisfies
    90	`d(u,v) ≤ diam(S)` by definition for any set; an earlier draft's
    91	connectedness hypothesis was retired in review as irrelevant),
    92	Hermitian `h_S` (same-support terms summed into one `h_S` before norms
    93	are taken), `H = Σ_S h_S`, with the supplied weighted activity
    94	
    95	> `κ := sup_x Σ_{S∋x} ||h_S|| · |S| · e^{μ·diam(S)} < ∞`
    96	
    97	for a supplied `μ > 0`, and `κ > 0` (at `κ = 0` every `h_S = 0`, `H = 0`,
    98	and every statement is trivial — excluded by convention as in the
    99	siblings). Volume-uniformity of the final constants means exactly this:
   100	they depend on `κ` and not otherwise on `Λ`, so a FAMILY of regions
   101	shares the bound iff it is supplied with a common `κ` (e.g.
   102	translation-invariant-bounded families). The `|S|` weight is
   103	load-bearing: it is what
   104	absorbs the contact-site choice in the peeling below. Observables `A`,
   105	`B` on disjoint supports `X`, `Y` (`d = d(X,Y) ≥ 1`, the standing
   106	scoping hypothesis), on either the tensor-product class (arbitrary
   107	finite site dimensions, as in the plaquette sibling) or the even-CAR
   108	class (even Hermitian `h_S`; block04 conventions, including the
   109	explicit odd-odd zeroth term). Heisenberg convention, the declared
   110	finite-matrix ODE context, and directed time with the `H → −H`
   111	extension, all unchanged from the siblings; the Duhamel layer (Jacobi,
   112	boundary reduction with **consecutive** overlap — each iterate's
   113	reduced generator sums over supports meeting the *previous term only*,
   114	which is exactly the siblings' per-term re-derivation — self-drop, norm
   115	transport, iterated integrals `|t|^k/k!`) is cited to the siblings
   116	where natively gated and consumed here as the unrolled series. The
   117	axioms supply no dynamics (needled). No literature statement is
   118	load-bearing. Workhorse disclosure: this block used two Opus 4.8
   119	max-effort workers (scout and math build) under the workhorse skill's
   120	substitution clause; both were graded against supervisor derivations
   121	recorded in the loop pack **before** reading worker output, and every
   122	load-bearing fact below is gated natively in the runner.
   123	
   124	## Results
   125	
   126	**Chain lemma (rebuilt).** For a chain `(S_1, …, S_k)` with
   127	`S_1 ∩ X ≠ ∅`, `S_{j+1} ∩ S_j ≠ ∅`, `S_k ∩ Y ≠ ∅`: by induction every
   128	site of `S_j` lies within `Σ_{i≤j} diam(S_i)` of `X` (anchor in the
   129	overlap, triangle inequality, ambient diameter), so
   130	
   131	> `Σ_{j=1}^{k} diam(S_j) ≥ d`.
   132	
   133	Gated by enumeration on explicit mixed-size families. Hence the weight
   134	split: `Π_j ||h_{S_j}|| ≤ e^{−μd} · Π_j (||h_{S_j}|| e^{μ diam S_j})`.
   135	
   136	**Single-step meeting bound and the peeling (the `|S|` bookkeeping).**
   137	For a fixed `S`, summing the site-weighted value
   138	`w*(S') := ||h_{S'}|| |S'| e^{μ diam S'}` over all `S'` meeting `S`:
   139	
   140	> `Σ_{S'∩S≠∅} w*(S') ≤ Σ_{x∈S} Σ_{S'∋x} w*(S') ≤ |S| · κ`,
   141	
   142	with strict inequality whenever some `S'` with `w*(S') > 0` meets `S`
   143	in two or more sites (the union bound counts it once per contact site — this exact
   144	slack is exhibited below on bonds: `12` versus the true `11`). The
   145	back-to-front peeling then bounds the full chain sum: the innermost
   146	tail obeys `U_k(S_{k−1}) ≤ |S_{k−1}| κ`, and at each outward step the
   147	`|S_j|` handed up combines with the plain weight
   148	`w(S_j) = ||h_{S_j}||e^{μ diam S_j}` to reconstitute exactly
   149	`w*(S_j) = |S_j| w(S_j)` — the object the meeting bound knows how to
   150	sum. No `|S|` factor is created or lost; by downward induction
   151	
   152	> `Σ_chains Π_j w(S_j) ≤ n_X^w · κ^{k−1} ≤ |X| · κ^k`,
   153	> `n_X^w := Σ_{S∩X≠∅} w*(S) ≤ |X|κ`,
   154	
   155	and with the weight split, the plain-norm chain sum obeys
   156	`Σ_chains Π_j ||h_{S_j}|| ≤ e^{−μd} n_X^w κ^{k−1}`. Gated two ways: the
   157	reconstitution identity symbolically, and the full chain sum versus the
   158	bound by exact enumeration on a finite mixed family.
   159	
   160	**Theorem (weighted quasilocal all-time volume-uniform Lieb-Robinson
   161	bound).** Feeding the counting layer into the siblings' unrolled series
   162	(prefactor `2||A||`, interior `2^{k−1}`, base `2||h_{S_k}||||B||` —
   163	total `2^{k+1}` at order `k`, cross-checked by two groupings) and
   164	resumming exactly (`2^{k+1}κ^{k−1}|t|^k = (2/κ)(2κ|t|)^k`):
   165	
   166	> `||[τ_t(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X^w/κ) · e^{−μd} ·
   167	> (e^{2κ|t|} − 1)`
   168	> `≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| |X| · e^{−μd} · (e^{2κ|t|} − 1)`,
   169	
   170	for all `t` and every finite `Λ`, with constants depending only on
   171	`||A||`, `||B||`, `n_X^w ≤ |X|κ`, `κ`, `μ`, `d` — not on `|Λ|` and not
   172	on the site dimensions. `||[A, B]|| = 0` on the tensor class and on the
   173	even sector of the CAR class; for odd-odd CAR pairs the zeroth term is
   174	kept exactly as in the block04 sibling. Sanity, gated: at `t = 0` the
   175	bound is tight (`e^0 − 1 = 0`); the `k = 1` series coefficient matches
   176	the direct order-1 term. Velocity readout: `v ≤ 2κ/μ` — **not claimed
   177	sharp** (see the slack ladder below), and for odd-odd CAR pairs it
   178	controls the dynamical tail only: the zeroth term `||[A, B]||` is `t`-
   179	and distance-independent, exactly as the block04 sibling states.
   180	
   181	**Consistency reduction and the slack ladder (worst-case envelope,
   182	enumerated).** On the strict bond class (`||h_b|| ≤ J`): the hypothesis
   183	gives `κ ≤ 12Je^μ` (`6` bonds per site × `|b| = 2` × `e^μ`), with
   184	EQUALITY exactly for the saturated bulk model (a region with an
   185	interior site whose six incident bonds all carry norm `J` — gated);
   186	a smaller family has smaller `κ` (a single bond gives `2Je^μ`). At the
   187	envelope, the rate is `24Je^μ` versus the direct sibling's `20Je^μ` —
   188	the worst-case comparison is **weaker by exactly `6/5`**, same shape.
   189	The slack decomposes exactly and is gated by enumeration: the meeting
   190	bound's union count gives `12` where the true count of distinct bonds
   191	meeting a bond is `11` (self double-counted at both endpoints), and the
   192	sibling's Duhamel self-drop removes the self term for `10`. The direct
   193	finite-range siblings remain sharper on their classes and are not
   194	modified; this theorem's value is the class, not the constant.
   195	
   196	**Instance families with closed-form `κ` (exact).** Pair interactions
   197	`h_{x,y} = J_0 λ^{|x−y|} K` (`K` a fixed two-site Hermitian of norm 1,
   198	`λ` rational, `ρ := λe^μ < 1`): with the `l1` sphere count on `Z^3`
   199	`N_3(r) = 4r^2 + 2` (gated by enumeration at two radii),
   200	
   201	> `κ_3D = 4J_0 · ρ(3 + ρ^2)/(1 − ρ)^3`,  `κ_1D = 4J_0 · ρ/(1 − ρ)`,
   202	
   203	via the geometric identities `Σ r ρ^r = ρ/(1−ρ)^2`,
   204	`Σ r^2 ρ^r = ρ(1+ρ)/(1−ρ)^3` and the exact numerator algebra
   205	`4ρ(1+ρ) + 2ρ(1−ρ)^2 = 2ρ(3+ρ^2)` (gated symbolically); rational
   206	instances gated by partial-sum-plus-tail brackets: `κ_3D = 14J_0` at
   207	`ρ = 1/3`, `κ_3D = 684J_0` at `ρ = 3/4`, `κ_1D = 4J_0` at `ρ = 1/2`.
   208	Pair supports `{x, y}` at distance `r ≥ 2` are disconnected sets —
   209	admissible, since connectedness was retired. The landed free-bilinear
   210	note's `W_mu` bound is the pair-support instance of this shape at
   211	`U = 1` under the map `κ = 2W_mu` (rates matching as `2κ = 4W_mu`) —
   212	needled as landed, not re-proved.
   213	
   214	**Fermionic lift (nearest-neighbor restriction removed).** The block04
   215	sibling's graded locality lemma is support-size-blind: even elements of
   216	disjoint support commute regardless of range. Hence the boundary
   217	reduction and base-term vanishing hold verbatim for weighted long-range
   218	even families, and the theorem's CAR form follows with no new algebra.
   219	Re-gated on a long-range instance: the distant even pair term
   220	`c_0^† c_3 + c_3^† c_0` commutes exactly with the intermediate odd
   221	generators and even density, and the weighted reduction
   222	`[H, n_0] = [Σ_{S∋0} h_S, n_0]` holds on a mixed NN-plus-long-range
   223	family.
   224	
   225	**Disposition of the pure-exponential no-go (load-bearing).** The
   226	exp-decay note proves: the reproducing ratio
   227	`Σ_z G_μ(d(x,z))G_μ(d(z,y))/G_μ(d(x,y)) ≥ R + 1` — pure-exponential
   228	weights have no finite reproducing constant, and its own many-body
   229	bound therefore uses a polynomial-corrected weight. **That obstruction
   230	does not bind this note's route:** here the decay `e^{−μd}` is
   231	extracted from each chain **before** any summation over intermediate
   232	supports (the weight split), and the peeling sums only the site-weighted
   233	activity per step — the reproducing ratio is never formed. The
   234	free-bilinear note's landed pure-exponential bound is the existing
   235	instance of exactly this move. This disposition is the reason a
   236	pure-exponential `|S|`-weighted hypothesis is admissible where the
   237	reproducing method fails; both of that note's relevant displays are
   238	needled.
   239

    14	# Microcausality: Gauged-Kernel Weighted-Activity Feed (Fixed Background)
    15	
    16	**Date:** 2026-07-18
    17	**Type:** bounded_theorem
    18	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
    19	**Scope:** bridge-conditional identification step at fixed gauge
    20	background; the one-particle kernel bound is supplied by the cited CT
    21	note (not re-proved); the axioms supply no dynamics.
    22	**Audit-status authority:** independent audit lane only. This note sets
    23	no audit verdict and predicts none.
    24	**Primitive status:** no primitive is approved, registered, edited, or
    25	enlarged here.
    26	**Primary runner:**
    27	[`scripts/microcausality_gauged_kernel_weighted_activity_feed_2026_07_18.py`](../scripts/microcausality_gauged_kernel_weighted_activity_feed_2026_07_18.py)
    28	**Runner cache:**
    29	[`logs/runner-cache/microcausality_gauged_kernel_weighted_activity_feed_2026_07_18.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/microcausality_gauged_kernel_weighted_activity_feed_2026_07_18.txt)
    30	
    31	## Purpose
    32	
    33	The CT note
    34	[`GAUGED_LOG_TRANSFER_QUASILOCALITY_COMBES_THOMAS_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-13.md`](GAUGED_LOG_TRANSFER_QUASILOCALITY_COMBES_THOMAS_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-13.md)
    35	proves a one-particle kernel bound at fixed gauge background —
    36	`|| <x| h[U] |y> || <= Const(m, d) e^{-gamma_CT ||x - y||_inf}` with
    37	"both `gamma_CT` and `Const` independent of the background `U` and of
    38	the volume" — and names its open item (iii): the full many-body
    39	fermionic Lieb-Robinson lightcone needs a separate quasilocal-LR
    40	composition step. The block07 sibling
    41	[`MICROCAUSALITY_WEIGHTED_QUASILOCAL_CLASS_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md`](MICROCAUSALITY_WEIGHTED_QUASILOCAL_CLASS_WALK_EXPANSION_LIEB_ROBINSON_BOUNDED_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-07-18.md)
    42	now supplies exactly the composition target: an all-time LR bound for
    43	any even-CAR interaction family with finite weighted activity `κ`.
    44	This note is the feed between the two: second-quantize the kernel,
    45	bound the resulting family's `κ` by an exact shell sum, and conclude
    46	that the fixed-background many-body bilinear log-transfer dynamics
    47	satisfies the block07 bound uniformly in the background. That takes the
    48	**fixed-background half** of the CT note's item (iii) **for the
    49	bilinear dynamics unconditionally, and for the many-body transfer
    50	operator itself conditionally on the explicit Gaussian-factorization
    51	hypothesis stated below** — the `U`-integrated / gauge-measure side
    52	remains open exactly as the CT note states it. The `U = 1` scalar
    53	case recovers the same scalar-bilinear applicability pattern as the
    54	landed free-bilinear note (its carrier conventions differ in detail;
    55	no carrier match is claimed); this note's delta is the gauged,
    56	background-uniform case, obtained purely by feeding the supplied
    57	gauged kernel bound through the same shell arithmetic.
    58	
    59	## Hypotheses (all supplied, none derived)
    60	
    61	The supplied objects: (i) the CT note's kernel bound at fixed
    62	background — `||<x|h[U]|y>|| ≤ K e^{−η||x−y||_∞}` with `K =
    63	Const(m,d)`, `η = gamma_CT`, both independent of `U` and of volume
    64	(cited on that note's own surface, with its own provenance and audit
    65	status; **not re-proved here** — the Combes-Thomas argument is that
    66	note's content); (ii) the CAR algebra and second-quantization
    67	conventions of the fermionic siblings (JW realization as the
    68	computational device); (iii) a supplied `mu` with `0 < mu <
    69	gamma_CT/3`; (iv) the block07 class definition and theorem, cited
    70	where natively gated. The second-quantized family is: for unordered
    71	pairs `{x, y}`, `x ≠ y`:
    72	
    73	> `h_{xy} = <x|h[U]|y> c_x^† c_y + <y|h[U]|x> c_y^† c_x`
    74	
    75	(Hermitian since the kernel matrix is), and on-site terms
    76	`h_x = <x|h[U]|x> c_x^† c_x`. Every term is **even** (two generators),
    77	so the block07 even-CAR form applies. **What the many-body object
    78	is, precisely (review-sharpened):** the theorem below concerns the
    79	second-quantized BILINEAR `H[U] = Σ_{x,y} <x|h[U]|y> c_x^† c_y` built
    80	from the supplied kernel — unconditionally. The further identification
    81	of this bilinear with the many-body log-transfer generator holds ONLY
    82	under an explicit additional supplied hypothesis, stated as such: a
    83	positive, number-conserving **Gaussian factorization**
    84	`T_MB[U] = C(U)·Γ(T_1[U])` with scalar `C(U) > 0` (then
    85	`−log T_MB = −log C(U)·1 + dΓ(−log T_1)`, and the scalar term is an
    86	identity shift that drops from every commutator — gated). A
    87	one-particle kernel alone does NOT imply the factorization (review
    88	counterexample: `Γ(e^{−h})·e^{−g n_1 n_2}` has the same one-particle
    89	restriction but a quartic log). Without the factorization hypothesis,
    90	only the bilinear theorem is claimed. The kernel here is treated with
    91	a **scalar fiber**; matrix-valued (internal-component) kernels need a
    92	fiber-dimension envelope, named open. The axioms supply no dynamics (needled). Workhorse disclosure: one Opus 4.8 max-effort verification
    93	worker (owner-directed substitution under the workhorse skill) checked
    94	the assembly line-by-line against supervisor ground truth recorded
    95	first in the loop pack; the runner gates everything natively.
    96	
    97	## Results
    98	
    99	**Term norms and parity (gated in the JW representation).**
   100	`||c_x^† c_y|| = 1` for `x ≠ y` and `||c_x^† c_x|| = 1` (partial
   101	isometries; exact operator norms gated), so by the triangle
   102	inequality
   103	
   104	> `||h_{xy}|| ≤ 2 |<x|h[U]|y>| ≤ 2K e^{−η||x−y||_∞}`,  `||h_x|| ≤ K`,
   105	
   106	and every term is even and Hermitian (gated).
   107	
   108	**Metric conversion and shell count (exact).** On `Z^3`:
   109	`||z||_1 ≤ 3 ||z||_∞` (per-coordinate: each of the three coordinates
   110	is at most the maximum; gated by enumeration with equality attained on
   111	the diagonal), so `e^{mu||z||_1} ≤ e^{3mu||z||_∞}`; and the `l_∞`
   112	sphere `{z : ||z||_∞ = r}` has exactly `(2r+1)^3 − (2r−1)^3 =
   113	24r^2 + 2` points (gated by enumeration at `r = 1, 2, 3`: 26, 98,
   114	218).
   115	
   116	**Activity bound (the feed).** For the block07 activity with the `l_1`
   117	ambient diameter, grouping the pair sum by `l_∞` shells and using the
   118	conversion:
   119	
   120	> `κ ≤ K + sup_x Σ_{y≠x} ||h_{xy}|| · 2 · e^{mu||x−y||_1}`
   121	> `≤ K + 4K Σ_{r≥1} (24r^2 + 2) x^r`,  `x := e^{−(gamma_CT − 3mu)}`,
   122	
   123	(the `4 = 2 × 2`: the Hermitian-pair norm bound times `|S| = 2`), and
   124	with the exact numerator identity
   125	`24x(1+x) + 2x(1−x)^2 = 26x + 20x^2 + 2x^3 = 2x(13 + 10x + x^2)`
   126	(gated symbolically),
   127	
   128	> `κ_U ≤ κ̄ := K + 8K · x(13 + 10x + x^2)/(1 − x)^3`,
   129	
   130	finite for every `mu < gamma_CT/3` (`x < 1`), where `κ_U` is the true
   131	activity of the background-`U` family and `κ̄` is the **common
   132	envelope**. At `x = 1/2` the envelope evaluates to exactly
   133	`κ̄/K = 585` (gated by a derived closed form plus a
   134	partial-sum-and-tail bracket); the envelope carries deliberate slack —
   135	for a scalar fiber the exact pair norm is `|k|`, not `2|k|`, so the
   136	sharper scalar bound is `1 + 2·146 = 293` (also gated) — `585` is the
   137	value of the stated envelope, not of `κ_U`. Because `κ̄` depends on
   138	the background only through `K` and `gamma_CT` — which the CT note
   139	supplies as `U`-independent — the envelope is
   140	**background-independent**.
   141	
   142	**Theorem (fixed-background many-body LR for the gauged bilinear
   143	generator).** For every fixed background `U`, the second-quantized
   144	family above lies in the block07 weighted quasilocal class with the
   145	`κ` bound displayed, so the block07 theorem applies verbatim: for
   146	disjoint `X`, `Y` and all `t`,
   147	
   148	> `||[τ_t^{U}(A), B]|| ≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| (n_X^w(U)/κ_U) ·
   149	> e^{−mu d} · (e^{2κ_U|t|} − 1)`
   150	> `≤ ||[A, B]|| + 2||A|| ||B|| |X| · e^{−mu d} · (e^{2κ̄|t|} − 1)`,
   151	
   152	the first line with the background's own constants, the second — the
   153	genuinely **uniform corollary** — with the single envelope `κ̄` and
   154	the coarse prefactor `|X|` shared by every background (the zeroth term
   155	vanishing when either observable is even, per the block04
   156	convention). This takes the **fixed-background half** of the CT
   157	note's open item (iii); the `mu < gamma_CT/3` threshold is the
   158	`d = 3` instance of the landed free-bilinear pattern
   159	`0 < d·mu < eta` (needled), and the `U = 1` scalar specialization recovers the same
   160	scalar-bilinear applicability pattern as the landed free-bilinear
   161	note (comparator; carrier conventions differ in detail and no
   162	carrier match is claimed).
   163	
   164	**Background-uniformity exhibit (exact, exhaustively enumerated).** On
   165	a 3-site chain with the toy `Z_2`-background kernel
   166	`h[s]_{xy} = s_{xy}·(1/2)^{|x−y|}` (`s_{xy} = s_{yx} ∈ {±1}`,
   167	`s_{xx} = 1`): all `2^3 = 8` sign backgrounds give identical pair
   168	norms `|h[s]_{xy}| = (1/2)^{|x−y|}`, hence identical activity — gated
   169	by full enumeration over backgrounds, with the box activity computed
   170	exactly at `e^{mu} = 9/8` under the note's envelope convention
   171	`||h_{xy}|| ≤ 2|k|` (the exact single-pair norm is `|k|`; using the
   172	envelope consistently makes the exhibit match the theorem's
   173	bookkeeping) (in one dimension `l_1 = l_∞`, so the
   174	exhibit incurs no conversion loss — stated, not hidden). The exhibit
   175	shows concretely what the uniformity claim uses: only the
   176	background-independent envelope of the kernel, never its signs.

     1	# Free Staggered Two-Step Dispersion in d Spatial Dimensions -- Narrow Theorem
     2	
     3	**Date:** 2026-06-12
     4	**Claim type:** bounded_theorem
     5	**Status authority:** independent audit lane only. This source note does not
     6	set or predict an audit outcome; later status is generated by the audit
     7	pipeline after independent review.
     8	**Primary runner:** [`scripts/free_staggered_two_step_dispersion_d_dimensional_2026_06_12.py`](../scripts/free_staggered_two_step_dispersion_d_dimensional_2026_06_12.py)
     9	**Cached output:** [`logs/runner-cache/free_staggered_two_step_dispersion_d_dimensional_2026_06_12.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/free_staggered_two_step_dispersion_d_dimensional_2026_06_12.txt)
    10	
    11	## Purpose
    12	
    13	This note supplies the missing d-dimensional free staggered two-step
    14	dispersion derivation used by the reconstructed-H quasilocality bridge.  It
    15	extends the same in-repo RP blocking construction from the one-spatial-axis
    16	authority to d spatial dimensions by keeping the staggered phase bookkeeping
    17	explicit.  The only d-dependent point is the spatial phase algebra; once it is
    18	folded into a two-site-cell basis, every reduced momentum sector has the same
    19	2 by 2 time-block algebra as the one-axis case with
    20	`sin p` replaced by the scalar `sqrt(sum_mu sin^2 p_mu)`.
    21	
    22	## One-hop Authorities
    23	
    24	- [`AXIOM_FIRST_RP_TWO_STEP_TRANSFER_MATRIX_POSITIVITY_NOTE_2026-05-28.md`](AXIOM_FIRST_RP_TWO_STEP_TRANSFER_MATRIX_POSITIVITY_NOTE_2026-05-28.md)
    25	  supplies the free staggered one-spatial-axis two-step blocking construction:
    26	  the alternating one-step matrices, the decaying two-step channel, and the
    27	  exact dispersion `E(p) = arcsinh(sqrt(m^2 + sin^2 p))`.
    28	- [`TRANSFER_MATRIX_LOG_QUASILOCALITY_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md`](TRANSFER_MATRIX_LOG_QUASILOCALITY_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-10.md)
    29	  supplies the one-axis sharp-rate analysis and the contour-shift
    30	  quasilocality mechanism.  This note restates the d-dimensional contour bound
    31	  below so no external rate constant is imported.
    32	
    33	Non-authority context pointers: `RECONSTRUCTED_H_QUASILOCAL_FROM_ANALYTIC_DISPERSION_MICROCAUSALITY_BRIDGE_NARROW_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-06-06.md`;
    34	`MICROCAUSALITY_FINITE_RANGE_H_AND_VLR_BRIDGE_THEOREM_NOTE_2026-05-09.md`.
    35	
    36	## Statement
    37	
    38	On the free `U = 1` staggered surface, with real `m > 0`, d spatial axes, even
    39	spatial periods, and the same two-step RP blocking in time, the
    40	action-derived two-step transfer is diagonal after the standard reduced
    41	momentum/two-site-cell transform.  For every full Brillouin-zone momentum
    42	`p in T^d`, the forward decaying one-particle eigenvalue is
    43	
    44	```text
    45	    lambda_-(p) = exp(-2 E_d(p)),
    46	    E_d(p) = arcsinh(sqrt(m^2 + sum_{mu=1}^d sin^2 p_mu)).
    47	```
    48	
    49	Equivalently, the reconstructed free log-transfer Hamiltonian has symbol
    50	`E_d(p)` up to the already declared blocked-time normalization convention.
    51	The formula is taste-degenerate across the `2^d` two-site-cell corners because
    52	`sin^2(k_mu + pi r_mu) = sin^2 k_mu`.
    53	
    54	For the position-space kernel
    55	
    56	```text
    57	    h(z) = (2 pi)^(-d) int_{T^d} E_d(p) exp(i p.z) d^d p,
    58	```
    59	
    60	the two-step symbol is `pi`-periodic in each momentum component, so
    61	`h(z) = 0` whenever any coordinate `z_mu` is odd.  Along coordinate axes the
    62	decay-rate scale is the one-axis value `arcsinh(m)`.  For arbitrary directions
    63	the general contour bound proved below gives, for every
    64	`0 < eta < arcsinh(m)`,
    65	
    66	```text
    67	    |h(z)| <= C_d(eta, m) exp(-eta ||z||_inf),
    68	    C_d(eta, m) = sqrt(m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2 eta).
    69	```
    70	
    71	Consequently, in the `l1` metric one may use any rate below
    72	`arcsinh(m)/d`; a concrete uniform choice is
    73	`r_d(m) = arcsinh(m)/(2d) > 0`.  The sharp anisotropic rate away from axes is
    74	left open.
    75	
    76	## Cell Convention and Phase Bookkeeping
    77	
    78	Use time coordinate `t` and spatial coordinate `x in Z^d`.  The canonical
    79	staggered phases are
    80	
    81	```text
    82	    eta_0(t,x) = 1,
    83	    eta_mu(t,x) = (-1)^(t + x_1 + ... + x_{mu-1}),      mu = 1,...,d.
    84	```
    85	
    86	Write
    87	
    88	```text
    89	    xi_mu(x) = (-1)^(x_1 + ... + x_{mu-1}),
    90	    H_hop = (1/2) sum_mu xi_mu(x) (tau_{+mu} - tau_{-mu}).
    91	```
    92	
    93	The spatial term on time slice `t` is `(-1)^t H_hop`, so the free mode
    94	equation has the same alternating form as the one-axis construction:
    95	
    96	```text
    97	    psi_{t+1} = -2 (m I + (-1)^t H_hop) psi_t + psi_{t-1}.
    98	```
    99	
   100	Thus
   101	
   102	```text
   103	    T_even = [[-2(m I + H_hop), I], [I, 0]],
   104	    T_odd  = [[-2(m I - H_hop), I], [I, 0]],
   105	    T_2    = T_odd T_even.
   106	```
   107	
   108	Now fold the spatial lattice by translations through two sites in every
   109	spatial direction.  A reduced momentum sector is labelled by
   110	`k in (-pi/2, pi/2]^d` and a cell/taste corner
   111	`r in {0,1}^d`, representing the full momentum
   112	`p_r = k + pi r`.  Multiplication by `xi_mu(x)` shifts the corner by
   113	`s_mu = (1,...,1,0,...,0)` with ones in slots `< mu`, and the finite
   114	difference contributes
   115	`i sin(p_{r,mu}) = i (-1)^{r_mu} sin(k_mu)`.  Therefore
   116	
   117	```text
   118	    H_hop(k) = i sum_mu sin(k_mu) Gamma_mu,
   119	    Gamma_mu |r> = (-1)^{r_mu} |r xor s_mu>.
   120	```
   121	
   122	A direct sign check gives
   123	
   124	```text
   125	    Gamma_mu^2 = I,
   126	    Gamma_mu Gamma_nu + Gamma_nu Gamma_mu = 0,     mu != nu.
   127	```
   128	
   129	Therefore
   130	
   131	```text
   132	    H_hop(k)^2 = - (sum_mu sin^2 k_mu) I.
   133	```
   134	
   135	This is the only dimension-dependent algebraic step.
   136	
   137	## Two-Step Eigenvalue Algebra
   138	
   139	Let `i lambda` be an eigenvalue of `H_hop(k)`, so
   140	`lambda^2 = sum_mu sin^2 k_mu`.  On that eigenline the time recurrence is
   141	identical to the one-spatial-axis recurrence with `sin p` replaced by
   142	`lambda`:
   143	
   144	```text
   145	    a = m + i lambda,
   146	    T_even(lambda) = [[-2a, 1], [1, 0]],
   147	    T_odd(lambda)  = [[-2conj(a), 1], [1, 0]].
   148	```
   149	
   150	The two-step matrix is
   151	
   152	```text
   153	    T_2(lambda) = T_odd(lambda) T_even(lambda)
   154	                = [[4|a|^2 + 1, -2conj(a)], [-2a, 1]].
   155	```
   156	
   157	It has determinant `1` and trace `2 + 4(m^2 + lambda^2)`.  With
   158	`R = m^2 + lambda^2` and `sinh E = sqrt(R)`,
   159	
   160	```text
   161	    spec T_2(lambda)
   162	      = { 1 + 2R + 2 sqrt(R(1+R)),
   163	          1 + 2R - 2 sqrt(R(1+R)) }
   164	      = { exp(+2E), exp(-2E) },
   165	    E = arcsinh(sqrt(R)).
   166	```
   167	
   168	Substituting `lambda^2 = sum_mu sin^2 k_mu` and unfolding
   169	`p = k + pi r` gives the displayed d-dimensional dispersion
   170	
   171	```text
   172	    E_d(p) = arcsinh(sqrt(m^2 + sum_mu sin^2 p_mu)).
   173	```
   174	
   175	The decaying forward channel is `exp(-2 E_d(p))`; the reciprocal growing
   176	channel is the backward-time solution, as in the one-axis construction.
   177	
   178	## Rate Bound and Axis Scale
   179	
   180	For `z != 0`, choose an axis `mu*` with `|z_mu*| = ||z||_inf` and shift only
   181	`p_mu*` to `p_mu* + i eta sign(z_mu*)`, with `0 < eta < arcsinh(m)`, while
   182	the transverse momenta remain real.  This is the sign compatible with the
   183	Fourier convention `exp(i p.z)`: the shifted exponential contributes
   184	`exp(-eta |z_mu*|)` on the selected axis.  On the shifted strip,
   185	
   186	```text
   187	    Re(m^2 + sum_mu sin^2 p_mu) >= m^2 - sinh^2 eta > 0,
   188	    |m^2 + sum_mu sin^2 p_mu| <= m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2 eta.
   189	```
   190	
   191	The principal square root and `arcsinh` branches are analytic there, and the
   192	same integral bound as the one-axis note gives
   193	
   194	```text
   195	    |E_d(p)| <= sqrt(m^2 + (d-1) + cosh^2 eta) = C_d(eta,m).
   196	```
   197	
   198	The contour shift then yields
   199	
   200	```text
   201	    |h(z)| <= C_d(eta,m) exp(-eta ||z||_inf).
   202	```
   203	
   204	Since `||z||_inf >= ||z||_1/d`, this also gives
   205	
   206	```text
   207	    |h(z)| <= C_d(eta,m) exp(-(eta/d) ||z||_1).
   208	```
   209	
   210	Choosing `eta = arcsinh(m)/2` gives the explicit positive all-direction
   211	`l1` rate `arcsinh(m)/(2d)`.  Along an axis, the first branch point is still
   212	at the transverse minimum `p_perp = 0`, `p_axis = i arcsinh(m)`; the local
   213	branch expansion is the same one-axis obstruction with transverse Laplace
   214	prefactors.  The runner verifies the axis rate against `arcsinh(m)` on
   215	resolved `d = 2` and `d = 3` blocks.  The sharp anisotropic rate for general
   216	directions is not asserted here.
   217	
   218	## Boundaries
   219	
   220	- Free `U = 1` staggered two-step sector only.
   221	- The theorem concerns the action-derived one-particle two-step transfer and
   222	  the corresponding free log-transfer symbol.  It does not prove gauged or
   223	  interacting log-transfer locality.
   224	- The kernel is quasilocal, not finite-range.  The even-offset rule is a
   225	  parity support rule, not compact support.
   226	- The all-direction statement is an explicit positive lower-bound rate.  The
   227	  sharp anisotropic rate away from coordinate axes remains an open target.
   228	- No literature values, external constants, empirical targets, or new
   229	  comparator numbers are imported.
   230	
   231	## Runner Checks
   232	
   233	The primary runner verifies:
   234	
   235	- the source note guardrails and one-hop markdown links;
   236	- the two-site-cell phase matrices in `d = 2` and `d = 3` satisfy the
   237	  Clifford relations;
   238	- `H_hop(k)^2 = -sum_mu sin^2(k_mu) I` on deterministic reduced-momentum
   239	  samples;
   240	- direct position-space construction of `T_odd T_even` on small `d = 2` and
   241	  `d = 3` tori has decaying spectrum matching `exp(-2E_d(p))` at machine
   242	  precision;
   243	- the Fourier kernel has only even spatial offsets;
   244	- the axis decay rate matches `arcsinh(m)` on resolved `d = 2` and `d = 3`
   245	  blocks;
   246	- the all-direction contour bound is respected on resolved finite blocks and
   247	  yields the concrete `l1` rate `arcsinh(m)/(2d)`.

   103	states the DEFINING property of the second quantization — vacuum

   127	  fixes only the scalar pair-measure normalization; the INTERTWINER

   129	  pinned the functor; corrected.

   133	  realized instance), multiplicativity **on commuting positive

   175	is). **Dimension scoping (worker-flagged, load-bearing):** the corner surface is `1+1d` (one

   186	> `0 < μ < min_k η_k`
   187

     9	**Cached runner output:** [`logs/runner-cache/axiom_first_rp_two_step_transfer_matrix_positivity.txt`](../logs/runner-cache/axiom_first_rp_two_step_transfer_matrix_positivity.txt)
    10	**Authority role:** standalone in-repo derivation note. It supplies the
    11	**free-case fermion-sector 2-step blocked transfer-matrix positivity** that the
    12	reflection-positivity row's 2-step formulation previously imported from the
    13	lattice literature (Sharatchandra-Thun-Weisz 1981 / Palumbo 2002 / Smit §6).
    14	The positivity is now **derived in-repo from the staggered action** for the
    15	free (`U = 1`) case, anchored to the exact free staggered dispersion, with the
    16	single-step transfer operator shown non-positive in the same construction. The
    17	fixed-`SU(3)` gauge-background extension of this same object is the
    18	separate fixed-background source note
    19	`RP_P2_GAUGE_EXTENSION_AND_REALIZATION_RESIDUAL_NOTE_2026-05-28.md`; audit
    20	status for that row is assigned only by the audit lane.
    21	
    22	## Claim
    23	
    24	On the free staggered-only action surface (`U = 1`, one Grassmann component per
    25	site, `1+1d`, `L_s` spatial sites, periodic, real mass `m > 0`), with the
    26	canonical staggered phases `eta_0 = 1` and `eta_1(t) = (-1)^t`:
    27	
    28	- **(P1) The single-step transfer operator is NOT positive.** The action-derived
    29	  single-step classical transfer matrices `T_even`, `T_odd` have genuinely
    30	  complex spectra for spatial momenta with `sin(p) != 0`; at the real-spectrum
    31	  exceptional modes `sin(p)=0` they have one negative eigenvalue. A single-step
    32	  transfer operator with complex spectrum, or with a negative real eigenvalue,
    33	  is not a positive operator.
    34	  This independently reproduces, on the transfer-matrix side, the single-step
    35	  spin-basis Lagrangian no-go of the reflection-positivity row (Gram min
    36	  eigenvalue `-0.80`).
    37	
    38	- **(P2) The 2-step blocked transfer matrix IS positive Hermitian.** The 2-step
    39	  blocked transfer matrix `T_hat^2 = T_odd . T_even` over two lattice spacings
    40	  is positive Hermitian on the free Fock space: `spec(T_hat^2) subset R_{>0}`,
    41	  `T_hat^2 = B^dag B`, and `H_hat = -log(T_hat^2) / (2 a_tau)` is self-adjoint
    42	  and bounded below by `0`. The single-particle 2-step kernel is the
    43	  action-derived decaying eigenvalue `t1^(2)(p) = e^{-2 E(p)}` with
    44	  `E(p) = arcsinh( sqrt(m^2 + sin^2 p) )` the exact free staggered dispersion,
    45	  so `H_hat = sum_p E(p) a_p^dag a_p >= 0`.
    46	
    47	The positive object is the **2-step** transfer matrix, not the single-step one;
    48	(P1) and (P2) are the two halves of the same in-repo construction. This note
    49	treats the free case explicitly and decisively. The fixed-`SU(3)` gauge
    50	extension is the separate fixed-background note named above; it is not
    51	re-derived here, and full `U`-integrated RP is not claimed here.
    52	
    53	Numbers (primary runner, `PASS=6 FAIL=0` at `m = 0.5`): 2-step decaying
    54	eigenvalue matches `e^{-2 E(p)}` over the Brillouin zone to max residual
    55	`2.9e-16` (C1, faithfulness); single-step spectra over `sin(p) != 0` have
    56	`min |Im eig(T_even/T_odd)| = 0.562` and the `sin(p)=0` modes have eigenvalues
    57	`{-1.618, +0.618}` at `m=0.5` (C2, non-positive); `T_hat^2` positive Hermitian
    58	with `min eig > 0` and exact
    59	`B^dag B` reconstruction `||T_hat^2 - B^dag B|| ~ 6e-17` for
    60	`L_s in {2, 3, 4, 6}` (C3); operator-picture 2-step Osterwalder-Schrader Gram
    61	Hermitian and PSD (`min eig = 0`) where the single-step naive Lagrangian Gram
    62	was `-0.80` (C4, cross-check); and the second-quantization functor verified
    63	in-repo from its defining creation-operator intertwiner, with
    64	`Gamma(t1^(2)) = exp(-2 a_tau H_hat)` to `~1e-16` (C5, functor check). The
    65	runner also verifies the finite spectral projectors of the action-derived
    66	`T_odd T_even`, selects the forward contraction channel `0<e^{-2E(p)}<=1`,
    67	and checks its exterior/Gamma image is positive `=B^dag B` (C6, decaying
    68	bridge). Faithfulness and 2-step positivity persist over
    69	the mass range `m in {0.05, 0.1, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0}` (max dispersion residual
    70	`6.1e-16`; `min eig(T_hat^2) > 0` throughout).
    71	
    72	## Why two steps (mechanism, not citation)
    73	
    74	With the canonical staggered phases `eta_0 = 1` and `eta_1(t) = (-1)^t` the
    75	temporal hop is clean but the spatial phase alternates with the time slice. The
    76	single-step transfer operator therefore alternates between two forms `T_even`
    77	(slices with `(-1)^t = +1`) and `T_odd` (slices with `(-1)^t = -1`); the
    78	physical object is `T_hat^2 = T_odd . T_even` over two lattice spacings. This is
    79	the same alternation that makes the single-step spin-basis Lagrangian Gram
    80	matrix non-PSD (reflection-positivity row, no-go runner, min eig `-0.80`); the
    81	single-step and 2-step surfaces are distinct, and only the 2-step object is
    82	positive. The lattice literature records the same standard 2-step staggered
    83	object (`Sharatchandra-Thun-Weisz 1981`, `Palumbo 2002`, `Smit §6`); here it is
    84	derived in-repo rather than cited as the load-bearing authority.
    85	
    86	## In-repo construction and proof (free case)
    87	
    88	### Step 1 — single-step transfer from the action
    89	
    90	For free staggered fermions in `1+1d` (one Grassmann component per site, `L_s`
    91	spatial sites, periodic) at fixed spatial momentum `p`, the staggered action's
    92	banded-in-time mode equation is
    93	
    94	```text
    95	    alpha_t psi_t + (1/2) psi_{t+1} - (1/2) psi_{t-1} = 0,
    96	    alpha_t = m + i eta_1(t) sin(p) = m + i (-1)^t sin(p),
    97	```
    98	
    99	which rearranges to `psi_{t+1} = -2 alpha_t psi_t + psi_{t-1}`, i.e. the
   100	single-step classical transfer matrix on the amplitude 2-vector
   101	`V_t = (psi_t, psi_{t-1})`:
   102	
   103	```text
   104	    V_{t+1} = T_s V_t,    T_s = [[ -2 alpha_s, 1 ], [ 1, 0 ]],
   105	    alpha_even = m + i sin(p),   alpha_odd = m - i sin(p).
   106	```
   107	
   108	`T_even`, `T_odd` come straight from the action; no convention is admitted.
   109	
   110	### Step 2 — single-step non-positivity (consistent with the no-go)
   111	
   112	For `sin(p) != 0`, `spec(T_even)` and `spec(T_odd)` are genuinely complex (off
   113	the positive real axis); the runner reports `min |Im eig(T_even/T_odd)| =
   114	0.562` over the non-exceptional Brillouin-zone modes at `m = 0.5`. At the
   115	real-spectrum exceptional modes `sin(p)=0` (`p=0`, and also `p=π` on even
   116	lattices), the one-step matrix is real symmetric with eigenvalues
   117	`-m-sqrt(m^2+1)<0` and `-m+sqrt(m^2+1)>0`; at `m=0.5` this is
   118	`{-1.618, +0.618}`. Thus the exceptional one-step channel is still
   119	non-positive. This independently reproduces the single-step no-go on the
   120	transfer-matrix side, and is the precise statement that the single-step (not
   121	the 2-step) object fails positivity.
   122	
   123	### Step 3 — dispersion anchor (faithfulness)
   124	
   125	The 2-step classical matrix `T2cl(p) = T_odd(p) . T_even(p)` has eigenvalues
   126	`{ e^{+2 E(p)}, e^{-2 E(p)} }` with
   127	
   128	```text
   129	    E(p) = arcsinh( sqrt( m^2 + sin^2 p ) ),    sinh^2 E(p) = m^2 + sin^2 p,
   130	```
   131	
   132	the exact free staggered `1+1d` dispersion. The decaying (physical) eigenvalue
   133	is `e^{-2 E(p)}`, real and positive. The runner verifies this match across the
   134	Brillouin zone with maximum residual `2.9e-16`. Reproducing the known
   135	dispersion is the proof that the construction is faithful to the staggered
   136	action, not an artifact: the 2-step block is the physical object whose spectrum
   137	is the staggered energy.
   138	
   139	### Step 3b — decaying-channel bridge to the one-particle kernel
   140	
   141	The passage from the action-derived classical monodromy to the one-particle
   142	positive-time transfer kernel is finite dimensional. For `m>0`, the two
   143	eigenvalues of `T2cl(p)` are reciprocal positive real numbers
   144	
   145	```text
   146	    lambda_-(p) = e^{-2E(p)} in (0,1],
   147	    lambda_+(p) = e^{+2E(p)} >= 1.
   148	```
   149	
   150	Their spectral projectors are explicit:
   151	
   152	```text
   153	    P_-(p) = (T2cl(p) - lambda_+(p) I) / (lambda_-(p) - lambda_+(p)),
   154	    P_+(p) = (T2cl(p) - lambda_-(p) I) / (lambda_+(p) - lambda_-(p)),
   155	```
   156	
   157	with `P_-^2=P_-`, `P_+^2=P_+`, `P_-P_+=0`, `P_-+P_+=I`, and
   158	`T2cl P_- = lambda_- P_-`. The positive-time coherent-state transfer is the
   159	stable half-line channel on `P_-`: a forward solution with any `P_+`
   160	component grows like `lambda_+^N` over `N` two-step blocks, so finite-action /
   161	finite-norm positive-time propagation sets that coefficient to zero. In the
   162	diagonal one-particle basis the forward kernel is therefore
   163	`K_2(p)=lambda_-(p)`. The growing reciprocal channel is the inverse
   164	backward-time solution, not the forward transfer kernel.
   165	
   166	For a one-mode coherent-state kernel
   167	`<bar z'|T_2|z> = exp(bar z' lambda_- z)`, the induced operator on the
   168	finite exterior algebra is exactly `diag(1,lambda_-)`; across momenta this is
   169	the wedge product `Gamma(K_2)`. The runner verifies the projector identities,
   170	the residual `T2cl P_- - lambda_- P_-`, the projector split/orthogonality, the
   171	finite exterior construction, the creation-operator intertwiner, and the
   172	`B^dag B` factorization at machine precision (C6). Thus
   173	`t1^(2)(p)=e^{-2E(p)}` is the action-derived decaying spectral channel, not a
   174	separate convention.
   175	
   176	### Step 4 — many-body 2-step positivity
   177	
   178	For a free (quadratic) fermion theory the many-body transfer operator is the
   179	second quantization `Gamma(t1)` of the single-particle transfer kernel `t1`.
   180	This is the standard free-fermion second-quantization functor for a quasi-free
   181	map (`Luscher 1977`; `Creutz 1977`; `Montvay-Munster Sec.4`; the underlying
   182	functor is `Shale-Stinespring` / `Berezin`); it is used here as a functorial
   183	relation, not as a positivity citation. For the diagonal free kernel of this
   184	construction the functor is elementary finite-dimensional linear algebra,
   185	recorded explicitly below and verified in-repo by the runner (C5), so the
   186	relation `Gamma(t1) = B^dag B` is **derived/checked in-repo, not asserted**.
   187	
   188	**The functor for a diagonal kernel (explicit, finite-dimensional).** The free
   189	theory factorizes across spatial momenta `p`, and the single-particle 2-step
   190	kernel `t1^(2)` is diagonal in those modes with eigenvalues
   191	`lambda_p = t1^(2)(p) = e^{-2 E(p)}` (real, positive, from Step 3b). The defining
   192	property of the second-quantization functor `Gamma` is that it carries a
   193	one-particle operator `K` (here diagonal, `K e_p = lambda_p e_p`) to the
   194	many-body operator `Gamma(K)` on the Fock space `H = tensor_p {|0>, |1>}` that
   195	fixes the vacuum and intertwines the creation operators,
   196	
   197	```text
   198	    Gamma(K) |vac> = |vac>,    Gamma(K) a_p^dag = lambda_p a_p^dag Gamma(K).
   199	```
   200	
   201	For a diagonal kernel these two requirements have a unique, explicit
   202	finite-dimensional solution: the per-mode tensor product, because each occupied
   203	mode `p` contributes a factor `lambda_p` and each empty mode contributes `1`,
   204	
   205	```text
   206	    Gamma(t1^(2)) = tensor_p diag( 1, lambda_p )
   207	                  = exp( -2 a_tau H_hat ),    H_hat = sum_p E(p) a_p^dag a_p,
   208	```
   209	
   210	an exact identity of finite matrices (`lambda_p = e^{-2 E(p)}` gives the second
   211	equality, so `Gamma` agrees with the number-operator exponential `exp(-2 a_tau
   212	H_hat)`; this is the relation `Gamma(e^{-h}) = e^{-d Gamma(h)}` specialized to
   213	the diagonal `h`). The runner builds `Gamma(t1^(2))` from its defining action on
   214	occupation-basis states, confirms it satisfies the creation-operator
   215	intertwiner `Gamma(K) a_p^dag = lambda_p a_p^dag Gamma(K)` mode-by-mode
   216	(`intertwiner err ~ 1e-18`), and checks it equals the matrix exponential
   217	`exp(-2 a_tau H_hat)` of the second-quantized `H_hat` assembled from
   218	Jordan-Wigner number operators (C5, `||Gamma - exp(-2 a_tau H_hat)|| ~ 1e-15`).
   219	So the functor relation `Gamma(t1) = B^dag B` is verified in-repo from the
   220	functor's defining intertwiner, not imported as a theorem.
   221	
   222	The single-particle 2-step kernel is the action-derived decaying spectral
   223	channel `t1^(2)(p) = e^{-2 E(p)}` from Step 3b, real and positive, so on the Fock space
   224	`H = tensor_p {|0>, |1>}` (dimension `2^{L_s}`)
   225	
   226	```text
   227	    T_hat^2 = Gamma( t1^(2) ) = tensor_p diag( 1, e^{-2 E(p)} )
   228	            = exp( -2 a_tau H_hat ),     H_hat = sum_p E(p) a_p^dag a_p.
   229	```
   230	
   231	Since `E(p) >= 0` for all `p`, `H_hat >= 0`, hence `T_hat^2` is positive
   232	Hermitian with `||T_hat^2|| = 1` (vacuum) and admits the explicit factorization
   233	
   234	```text
   235	    T_hat^2 = B^dag B,    B = exp( -a_tau H_hat ) = tensor_p diag( 1, e^{-E(p)} ).
   236	```
   237	
   238	This is exactly the 2-step reflection-positivity statement:
   239	`H_hat = -log(T_hat^2) / (2 a_tau)` is self-adjoint and bounded below by `0`.
   240	The runner builds `T_hat^2` from the action-derived classical 2-step eigenvalue
   241	(not posited), confirms the finite exterior bridge, positive Hermitian
   242	(`min eig > 0`), and the exact `B^dag B` reconstruction
   243	(`||T_hat^2 - B^dag B|| ~ 6e-17`) for `L_s in {2, 3, 4, 6}`, and over a mass
   244	range `m in [0.05, 5.0]`.
   245	
   246	### Cross-check (route R2 — 2-step OS Gram)
   247	
   248	The Osterwalder-Schrader reflected two-point correlator on the 2-step blocked
   249	surface, in the transfer-matrix representation, is
   250	`G(F_I, F_J) = <vac| F_I^dag T_hat^2 F_J |vac>` for second-quantized
   251	positive-time observables. This is the genuine OS Gram on the 2-step blocked
   252	surface; it is manifestly Hermitian and PSD iff `T_hat^2 >= 0`. The runner
   253	builds it explicitly on the Fock space (identity, single creation/annihilation,
   254	and pair observables) and confirms it is Hermitian with `min eig = 0` (PSD), in
   255	direct contrast to the single-step naive Lagrangian Gram (`min eig = -0.80`).
   256	The transfer-matrix route (Steps 1-4), the finite exterior-algebra bridge, and
   257	the OS-Gram route (R2) agree.
   258

import itertools, math, numpy as np
for L in (2,4,6,8,10,12):
  for m in (0.05,0.1,0.5,1.0,2.0):
    eta=0.9*math.asinh(m)
    C=math.sqrt(m*m+2+math.cosh(eta)**2)
    ps=[2*math.pi*n/L for n in range(L)]
    vals={}
    worst=(0,None,None)
    for z in itertools.product(range(L), repeat=3):
      s=0j
      for p in itertools.product(ps, repeat=3):
        E=math.asinh(math.sqrt(m*m+sum(math.sin(q)**2 for q in p)))
        s += E*np.exp(1j*sum(p[i]*z[i] for i in range(3)))
      h=abs(s/(L**3))
      dist=max(min(a,L-a) for a in z)
      ratio=h/(C*math.exp(-eta*dist))
      if ratio>worst[0]: worst=(ratio,z,h)
    if worst[0]>1+1e-9:
      print('VIOLATION',L,m,eta,C,worst)

     1	# Minimal Framework Axioms (Lattice, Qubit, Admissibility, Record)
     2	
     3	> **Key terms used in this doc** are indexed A-Z at `docs/KEY_TERMINOLOGY.md`;
     4	> each row points to the canonical source-of-truth doc.
     5	
     6	**Date:** 2026-06-29
     7	**Type:** meta
     8	**Status:** current public framework axiom memo for the qubit-on-`Z^3`
     9	package with local admissibility and fixed scalar record readout.
    10	**Status authority:** explicit owner approval for the 2026-06-29 foundation
    11	reset is recorded in `docs/audit/AXIOM_MINIMALITY_POLICY.md` section 6.
    12	Audit status remains set only by the independent audit lane.
    13	
    14	**Supersedes:** `MINIMAL_AXIOMS_2026-06-05.md`. The 2026-06-05 memo remains the
    15	historical source for the prior Lattice/Quantum/Record wording in which
    16	realized outcome was latent in Record and no explicit local admissibility
    17	constraint was named.
    18	
    19	## Purpose
    20	
    21	This note states the framework's minimal ontology premises. They are named
    22	rather than treated as bare letter codes:
    23	
    24	1. **Lattice**
    25	2. **Qubit**
    26	3. **Admissibility**
    27	4. **Record**
    28	
    29	Legacy `A1`/`A2` numbering and the older `Quantum` axiom name are historical.
    30	New repo surfaces should use the axiom names above unless quoting an older
    31	document.
    32	
    33	## The Four Framework Axioms
    34	
    35	### Lattice / Physical Locality
    36	
    37	Physical sites are the points of the cubic lattice `Z^3`, with nearest-neighbor
    38	adjacency, standard translations, and proper cubic rotations about each site.
    39	
    40	No site is privileged. Sites are distinguished by the supplied lattice
    41	structure alone.
    42	
    43	### Qubit / Site Possibility
    44	
    45	Each site has a domain of local possibilities.
    46	
    47	The full one-site possibility domain has algebraic presentation `M_2(C)`.
    48	
    49	A `Cl(3,0)`-compatible real-algebra presentation may be used equivalently and
    50	adds no further primitive structure.
    51	
    52	No possibility is privileged. Possibilities are distinguished by the supplied
    53	algebraic structure alone.
    54	
    55	### Admissibility / Local Constraint
    56	
    57	There is one fixed nearest-neighbor admissibility rule, covariant under lattice
    58	translations and proper cubic rotations.
    59	
    60	For each site, the available possibilities are determined by, and vary with,
    61	the nearest-neighbor conditions.
    62	
    63	### Record / Fixed Reality
    64	
    65	Records form.
    66	
    67	When present, a record locks exactly one admissible local possibility. A
    68	site never carries more than one record; records are permanent.
    69	
    70	Only records are readable. A readout value is determined by record content
    71	alone. For any finite collection of pairwise-disjoint records, scalar readout
    72	`I` is additive, with `I(empty)=0`.
    73	
    74	## Qualification
    75	
    76	These axioms state only their named primitive content. Further physical
    77	structure requires a retained derivation or bridge, or explicit approved-
    78	primitive registration, before use as a premise. A choice not fixed by the
    79	supplied structure remains a named conditional or open dependency.
    80	
    81	A state is a configuration of records.
    82	
    83	A law privileges no states. Its domain is a supplied condition, and at every
    84	state where the condition holds it gives exactly one answer.
    85	
    86	## Audit-Pipeline Treatment
    87	
    88	The machine-readable source of this axiom set is the stable `minimal_axioms`
    89	entry in `docs/audit/data/axiom_premise_nodes.json`. Dependencies on
    90	`minimal_axioms` chain-satisfy without making downstream rows
    91	`retained_bounded`.
    92	
    93	**Primary runner:** `scripts/audit_companion_minimal_axioms_clean_base_exact.py`
    94	checks only elementary algebra/notation sanity for the four axiom names. It
    95	does not derive or enlarge the axiom set.
    96	
    97	Axioms and approved primitives are the complete supplied foundation.
    98	Depending on the Lattice, Qubit, Admissibility, or Record axiom, or on an
    99	explicitly approved primitive such as `scale_reference_primitive`, must not be
   100	treated as a source of bounded status. No admission class exists: every other
   101	scientific dependency must be retained-derived or remain conditional/open.
   102	
   103	## Relation To Dynamics And Kinetic Branch Selection
   104	
   105	Admissibility is not a dynamics axiom. It determines availability by a
   106	nearest-neighbor rule: for each site, the available possibilities are
   107	determined by, and vary with, the nearest-neighbor conditions. It does not
   108	choose a Hamiltonian or transfer operator, supply transition probabilities or
   109	weights, select a scalar or nonzero kinetic branch, assert a Dirac-square
   110	carrier, define a time metric, or provide a record-production process or
   111	physical persistence dynamics.
   112	
   113	Static spatial kinetic questions, probability/process questions, and temporal
   114	evolution questions should be tracked separately. A realized kinetic branch, if
   115	proposed, is downstream content: it needs a retained derivation or bridge, or
   116	an approved-primitive registry update, before audit rows may use it
   117	as load-bearing content. The four axioms are compatible with such later
   118	content, but do not include it.
   119	
   120	## Relation To The Older Observable-Principle Parent
   121	
   122	`OBSERVABLE_PRINCIPLE_FROM_AXIOM_NOTE.md` is not this axiom note and is not an
   123	approved axiom-premise node. It remains a broader conditional parent that
   124	packages additive scalar observables with further readout/log-det/modulus
   125	structure. That older parent must not be moved wholesale into
   126	`docs/audit/data/axiom_premise_nodes.json`.
   127	
   128	Rows that require only the Record axiom should cite this minimal-axiom
   129	authority. Rows that require P2/modulus, log-det, source/action, measurement,
   130	Born weights, readout-context selection, central-sector decomposition, `K`/CPT
   131	structure, transition relations, record-production dynamics, physical
   132	persistence dynamics, local observability, or any other additional bridge must
   133	cite separate retained authorities or remain bounded/pending according to the
   134	audit ledger.
   135	
   136	## Relation To The 2026-06-05 Record Wording
   137	
   138	The 2026-06-05 Record axiom named durable realized-outcome registration and
   139	gave a `K`/CPT orbit reading once a finite central-sector readout context and
   140	fixed `K`/CPT conjugation were supplied. This reset separates generic site
   141	possibility, local admissibility, and fixed records from context-specific
   142	readout structure:
   143	
   144	- Qubit names the domain of local possibilities and its full one-site algebraic
   145	  presentation.
   146	- Admissibility names the nearest-neighbor rule by which, for each site, the
   147	  available possibilities are determined by, and vary with, the
   148	  nearest-neighbor conditions.
   149	- Record names the fixed locking of one admissible local possibility,
   150	  one-record-per-site uniqueness, permanence, and finite scalar readout
   151	  additivity over disjoint record collections.
   152	- `K`/CPT orbit structure, central-sector decomposition, and any sector
   153	  generation rule are downstream readout-context content, not generic axiom
   154	  content.
   155	
   156	## Open Gates Outside The Axioms
   157	
   158	The four axioms do not close, import, or rename the framework's downstream open
   159	gates. In particular, the following remain outside axiom content:
   160	
   161	- the staggered-Dirac/finite-Grassmann realization and `AC_phi_lambda`;
   162	- the strong-CP theta gauge and mass-side derivation obligations;
   163	- P2/modulus/phase-blindness and any log-det readout theorem;
   164	- context selection, measurement basis selection, Born weights, probability
   165	  rules, update laws, decoherence mechanisms, and formation rules (which
   166	  admissible possibility a new record locks, at which site, with what weight,
   167	  or at what rate);
   168	- arrow, record-production dynamics, physical persistence dynamics, time metric,
   169	  and local observability of records;
   170	- source/action and physical-observable identification;
   171	- `g_bare = 1` convention handling;
   172	- the scale-reference primitive and the separate gravity self-consistency
   173	  question that the framework's natural unit equals the Planck length.
   174

   177	The April 15 through May 20 sequence separated the one-qubit local algebra and
   178	the `Z^3` lattice from downstream realization gates that had previously been
   179	written too axiomatically. The 2026-06-04 memo added scalar finite Record
   180	additivity as the third explicitly approved premise. The 2026-06-05 memo refined
